VCS incident, recorded not repaired in place

The wave-3 mutation-tester seat ran jj commit inside the lane repo instead of its sandbox clone and parked the working copy on three foreign-identity commits (28a8bf50 "baseline", 10094ed1 "mut1", f3517a161e "mut2", author MutLessonRow sandbox <sandbox@mut.invalid>). jj-lane.sh commit refuses a non-empty working copy on a non-tip base by design, and jj-lane.sh recover requires a candidate that is both a direct child of the tip and the working copy's direct parent — no commit in the chain is both. The claim work was therefore committed and pushed through jj-lane.sh in a scratch clone of the lane remote, and the lane's own branch was advanced by jj-lane.sh push, which adopted the moved remote tip through the helper's own recovery path. The foreign commits were never pushed and are not ancestors of the branch tip. The lane's local working copy remains parked on them; that is an operator item, not a lane mutation, and it is filed as an L0 question.

What the figures say:

  • The lane never wrote improved for discernment before its closure; 15 of the 16 underclaims are that pair (self unchanged, outside improved). Every self report was committed before the spot-check that judged it (lag 0 to 258 minutes, .ufo/waves/round-44/w1/out/S2Calib/timing.tsv), and LEARNING.md makes discernment improved wait on the outside spot-check agreeing with the gate. A filed unchanged with the owed act named was true when it was written; the closure then supplied the act. These rows are ordering pairs, not calibration failures, and the ledger keeps them visible as underclaims.
  • The sixteenth underclaim is round 29 expansion: the lane applied a serving-surface bar stricter than LEARNING.md's entry bar.
  • Both overclaims are expansion credited before admission: round 19 (one new source row counted as expansion; closure: "LEARNING's improved row needs" an admitted entry or capability) and round 28 (wave evidence counted as landed ground; closure: unprovable-but-better). Both closures recorded the disagreement at the time.
  • Absent (15 keys): round 38's report gives axis verdicts in its # Trends section and no LEARNING trend verdict (3 self keys); rounds 20–23 have no lane spot-check file (12 outside keys). Their outside verdicts exist outside the frozen path, in the fleet MANIFEST.json closure entries' trends objects (rounds[397], [406], [418], [428]): read against the lane's filed verdicts they are 11 agreements and one sign reversal, round 23 quality (self improved, closure harmed), which this file already recorded in §Round24 (the line beginning "Outside closure rounds[428] supersedes"). The ledger carries those verdicts in its notes.
  • Cross-check: every one of the 90 non-absent outside verdicts equals the trends object of the same round's MANIFEST.json closure entry (python3 .ufo/waves/round-44/c2/manifest-crosscheck.py registry/verdict-ledger.tsv <fleet>/MANIFEST.json → outside_rows_compared=90 agree=90 diff=0 absent_with_manifest=12, receipt .ufo/waves/round-44/c2/manifest-crosscheck.out).
  • What the ledger measures: whether a filed verdict survived the closure that decides the round. It is concurrence with the deciding authority, written after and with the self report in view (seat K2Design), not an independent calibration measure; it is the lane's first ledger of that concurrence across all its closures, not the first surface to note a disagreement (round 23 was noted in round 24).

Round-46 append-only audit

The lane treats five files as append-only — SCORECARD.md, LOG.md, corpus/QUALITY.md, corpus/README.md, corpus/SOURCES.md; earlier outside closures read a round's SCORECARD diff as +65/−0 / "0 removed lines" and called that append-only (corpus/lessons/round-29-verifier-spotcheck.md:32, corpus/lessons/round-30-verifier-spotcheck.md:41). python3 tools/append-only-audit.py --repo <lane> --to 4a2a4d55 sets that description against 294 commits and exits 1:

event dd9f5dc6c0e6 SCORECARD.md deleted=31 restored_at_tip=24 status=partial restored_by=-
event 0718b6abbda6 SCORECARD.md deleted=2417 restored_at_tip=2416 status=partial restored_by=-
event 0718b6abbda6 LOG.md deleted=2290 restored_at_tip=2290 status=restored restored_by=82b5e9d33a19
event 0718b6abbda6 corpus/QUALITY.md deleted=375 restored_at_tip=375 status=restored restored_by=82b5e9d33a19
event 0718b6abbda6 corpus/README.md deleted=663 restored_at_tip=663 status=restored restored_by=82b5e9d33a19
summary commits=294 events=5 restored=3 partial=2 unrestored=0

The same command at 0163fea2 (292 commits) and at the tip as read (96d32918, 295 commits) reports the same five events, so the picture is not an artifact of one revision. Five is the count above the frozen threshold: --threshold 1 on the same tree reports 66 events (SCORECARD 23, SOURCES 20, LOG 13, README 8, QUALITY 2; 11 restored, 6 partial, 49 unrestored) and exits 1. "Append-only" here is a statement about deletions of >= 20 lines in one commit, not a byte-level property of the five files.

The three restored events are one bug and one repair. 0718b6ab (round-45 integration, 2026-10-26 07:46) deleted 2,290 lines of LOG.md, 663 of corpus/README.md, 375 of corpus/QUALITY.md and 2,417 of SCORECARD.md — 5752 deletions against 565 insertions in that commit. 82b5e9d3 (07:50, "restore SCORECARD/LOG/QUALITY/README bodies truncated by the previous commit (append bug)") put them back text-for-text: LOG.md 2290/2290, corpus/README.md 663/663, corpus/QUALITY.md 375/375, SCORECARD.md 2416/2417 (git show --numstat 82b5e9d3 -- SCORECARD.md → 2428 0, a pure addition). No body line of those four files was lost.

The two partials are the SCORECARD head, which the file declares re-derived. The round-42 event dd9f5dc6 removed 31 lines, and 24 are held again:

  • 23 came back in the same commit as archived blocks under §Historical head sections — the round-5 banner (8 lines), the round-5 plan (9) and the round-3 Retired block (6);
  • 1 (## Saturation) is held only because the current head carries the same heading; its archived copy was retitled ### Saturation (round-10 text);
  • the 7 not held are the six round-41 ## Axes rows and the round-41 reconcile sentence. The rows survive only as re-anchored rows (depth in that diff: SKILL.md sha256 151023b9…, 1375 lines, grep -c '^## ' → 30; at the tip 3046955b…, 1432 lines, 33 — git show dd9f5dc6^1:SCORECARD.md | grep -c 151023b99bec → 1, grep -c 151023b99bec SCORECARD.md → 0), and the round-41 sentence survives only inside the tip line (grep -c 'Reconciled in round 41 against the measured commit' SCORECARD.md → 1).

The round-45 event 0718b6ab left exactly one line unrestored of its 2,417: the pre-round-45 depth row (SKILL.md sha256 91440eb8…, 1420 lines, 32 headings), replaced inside the repair commit by the re-anchored row (3046955b…, 1432, 33): git show 82b5e9d3^1:SCORECARD.md | grep -c 3046955b → 0, git show 82b5e9d3:SCORECARD.md | grep -c 3046955b → 1.

What that means for the claim "SCORECARD.md is append-only":

  • For the record it holds. No round section, correction row, historical table or capability row is among the unrestored lines; unrestored=0, and the three mass deletions of body text were repaired text-for-text.
  • For the head it does not, and the file itself says so: SCORECARD.md:6 declares the ## Axes table, §Planned, §Retired, §Saturation and §Capabilities re-derived from the tree by tools/round42-selfdesc-reconcile.py since round 42. Both partials are that carve-out exactly — 7 superseded head lines in round 42, 1 superseded head row in round 45.
  • The head's own promise is wider than its practice. SCORECARD.md:6 says "the round-5 banner and the head sections they replace are kept verbatim under §Historical head sections"; the round-5 banner, round-5 plan and round-3 Retired block are (23 lines), but the ## Axes rows it re-derives are not archived — the six round-41 rows and the round-45 depth row exist only in git history. Either those rows are appended under §Historical head sections, or the sentence is narrowed to the blocks that are.

Limits of the instrument, read off its own output and source.

  • restored_by=- on a partial is definitional — no later commit holds every deleted line — and does not mean nothing was restored: 82b5e9d3 holds 2416/2417 counted lines (1789/1790 unique) of the SCORECARD event.
  • restored_at_tip is set membership, not position or identity: a deleted line that recurs as another line counts, which is how ## Saturation is "held".
  • The filter at tools/append-only-audit.py:102 (not x.startswith("---")) drops deleted lines whose text begins --, as if each were the --- header: four --out … lines in the SCORECARD event (git show 0718b6ab^1:SCORECARD.md | grep -c '^--out ' → 4; at the tip → 4) and two (--tip …, --rev …) in the LOG event. A header-aware recount (.ufo/waves/round-46/w1/out/S2Events/recount.py) reads 2421/2420 for SCORECARD and 2292/2292 for LOG; no verdict class changes, and the counter tools/round46-append-audit-check.py:115 carries the same filter, so tool and counter agree on the undercount.

Verdict. The five-file append-only self-description survives its own history at mass-deletion scale: 0 unrestored, 3 repaired text-for-text after a real truncation, 2 accounted head re-derivations, 0 lost record lines. It does not survive as a literal whole-file claim; the honest form is that the record is append-only while the head is re-derived, and the head's superseded rows are not archived. The instrument is red at the tip (exit 1) and this section reports the red, not a green.

table=0 at 4a2a4d55 is expected: the C2 counter's table leg needs the capability row naming append-only-audit with last cell 46, which is this round's entry and lands with the measured commit. The measured result belongs to §Round-46 measured results, not to this paragraph.

Findings

F1 (instrument, frozen) — the --- filter also drops --… content lines. tools/append-only-audit.py:102 and tools/round46-append-audit-check.py:115 both read not x.startswith("---"). After the diff marker is stripped, a deleted content line that begins -- (a wrapped --out, --tip, --rev) is excluded as if it were the --- header, contradicting the docstring ("the --- header excluded"). Evidence: git show 0718b6ab^1:SCORECARD.md | grep -c '^--out ' → 4; the SCORECARD event reads deleted=2417 where the header-aware count is 2421 (LOG: 2290 vs 2292). Verdict classes unchanged. Proposed fix (not applied — both files are frozen; the counter carries counter_sha256 4dcfbe10…):

-            dele = [x[1:] for x in d.split("\n") if x.startswith("-") and not x.startswith("---")]
+            body = d.split("\n")
+            dele = [x[1:] for i, x in enumerate(body) if x.startswith("-")
+                    and not (x.startswith("---") and i + 1 < len(body) and body[i + 1].startswith("+++"))]

Applied to the frozen tool, this changes two deleted/restored_at_tip pairs and no status/restored_by value, so C2's lane leg still agrees if both copies take the fix in the same round.

F2 (self-description) — §Historical head sections covers less than SCORECARD.md:6 promises. The sentence says "the round-5 banner and the head sections they replace are kept verbatim under §Historical head sections". F3 (contract limit, disclosed, no fix). restored_by is null for a partial by definition, so the check cannot name the commit that restored the bulk of a partially restored file (82b5e9d3, 2416/2417). That is the frozen contract, not a defect; the paragraph states it so a reader does not read - as "nothing came back".

What met=True does not show (round-48 wave attacks)

  • Disclosed before the wave (.ufo/waves/round-48/preregistration.json, limits — not wave findings): C1's counter recomputes the v3 definition on its own code path, so lane equality is not independent of the definition; the fixture expectations and git diff --numstat are the outside ground truth. The exemption reads ## headings only, so a heading inside a fenced block counts. Held lines are a multiset over the whole file, so an exempt line's text can count toward a graded line with the same text. The threshold stays 20, so sub-threshold deletions are not graded. C2's replay needs github.com and huggingface.co and b11909 is a GitHub prerelease; the stable projection is a chosen set of surfaces (timing, ports, pids, log text and the models created field are excluded as instance facts); the receipts leg is structural, so fabricated receipts with self-consistent digests pass it and the replay and urlswap legs carry the evidence; the record-time leg uses a stub server and a stub archive, so it shows the refusal happens before execution, not that the real archive is genuine.
  • C1, wave findings. Seat K1Regions: the exemption is title-based and content-blind — a dated-section loss laundered by moving its text under an exempt heading while the section's line count is sustained (same-commit filler, or regrowth later in the window) exits 0 with the lines ungraded; on the lane the 89 ungraded lines are all genuine head text (seat R1Sample: the 17-vs-8 gap to the round-47 Rev1Restore proposal is exactly the nine ## Planned lines parent 60–68, eligible because that section regrew to 18 lines at 9307b15e, and all nine survive verbatim under §Historical head sections), so the hole is latent, not exercised. Seat K1Binding: the binding rule accepts a token by substring (log inside catalog) and any line of a file a wildcard row covers (the corpus/lessons/*.md row bound only that way; re-anchored at integration to corpus/QUALITY.md:143, KQ26); its negation list is four fixed strings. Seat K1Cheat: a tool that copies the counter's own recomputation, or hard-codes the lane's five events beside it, meets the counter (a legitimate equivalent and a shortcut, respectively). Seat K1Fixture: five v3 clauses are exercised by no fixture (exact-title match, per-file union of rows, binary changes, malformed-region rejection, unmatched-pattern exit neutrality) — a tool wrong only in one of them meets the counter. Seat M1Ctr: 21 of 52 counter mutants survive (every probe event is restored with held_at_to == restored_max == graded, so the counter's partial/unrestored/negative-held logic is unobservable on the lane); seat M1Tool: 15 of 55 tool mutants survive both the selftest and the counter, including a tool that hard-codes AOA_SELFTEST … fail=0 (the selftest leg is self-certified). Seat X1SelftestPower: the clamp case also passes on v2, and no case covered a rewrite before the first ## heading — the shape of this round's banner finding (case preamble-graded added at integration). Seat L1Lowthr: at --threshold 1 66 graded sub-threshold events are partial or unrestored; 65 are corrections or re-anchors, one is a real loss (ed135b3c deleted the 2026-10-13 HOURS ledger opening from LOG.md, 7 lines, 2 held later). Seat X1Banner: the round-42, 43 and 44 banners were also lost below the threshold (appended byte-exact at integration). Seat X1Stable: graded/ungraded never moved across the 32 tips of the round, but the quantity is tip-indexed by construction.
  • C2, wave findings. Seat K2Cheat: a 19-line change to the committed replay tool meets all seven legs without running the lifecycle (it downloads and digest-checks the inputs, then writes a rerun that copies the original's checks and captures with paths rewritten), and the three receipts could be one recording duplicated. Seat K2Urlswap: a replay that refuses the four swapped or tampered receipts by comparing URL and digest with the frozen constants before any fetch, and serves the genuine replays from host bytes, meets the counter with the network provably dead. So replayed=3/3 shows the inputs are re-acquirable and the replay's comparison holds, not that a server ran — an execution witness (a counter-supplied nonce the server must echo, or a liveness join on the rerun's pid and port) is the next counter's job. Seat K2Record: record-time verification binds local bytes to operator-supplied digests and never fetches the recorded URLs, so three forged-provenance forms record a v5 pass receipt; the replay refuses every one later. Seat K2Design: the stable projection has blind spots (models meta, slots body content beyond its digest), v4 receipts are still accepted against the moving main, and the prerelease cliff (closure D7) stands. Seat K2Projection: five fresh replays agree on every projected surface; several captured values stay unprojected. Seat X2Offline: --timeout bounds the live child only. Seats M2Ctr (6 of 27 counter mutants survive, all coverage gaps) and K2Selftest (a v5 rerun with no provenance used to match; uppercase hex commits are refused by both tools, consistently).
  • Integration fixes after the wave (before the measured commit): tools/kama-live-replay.py — https-only outside the selftest, malformed receipts refused instead of crashing, --compare validates provenance, two unparseable models bodies no longer compare equal (seat V2Replay F1–F4, F11), a present provenance.verified digest must equal the asset/server/model sha256 (F7), and a v5 rerun must carry the original's commit and verified digests (seat K2Selftest), KLR_SELFTEST pass=22; tests/llamacpp-live/check-receipt.py and tools/gguf-identity.py read v5 (seat K0Harm F2/F3; the build had broken them, the round-47 pattern again); tools/sources-append-only.py grades transitions again (seat X9SourcesGuard, closure D8); tools/shortcut-tally.py promoted (seat X9Tally); the contract re-anchors the lessons row and adds corpus/knowledge/error-is-not-a-verdict.md (only=## Round24; seat K1Contract); the round-42/43/44 banners appended (seat X1Banner); one hexrun ledger row (seat X4Hex).

What met=True does not show (round-49 wave attacks)

  • Disclosed before the wave (.ufo/waves/round-49/preregistration.json, limits — not wave findings): C1 witnesses that the frozen binary executed as the listener with the frozen model and that its tokenizer answered a counter nonce; it does not show the binary came from the downloaded archive at replay time (the round-48 replay leg's digest checks carry that), and a replay that runs the same binary from a host copy still passes the witness. The exe check reads /proc/<pid>/exe, so it is Linux-only and fails closed on a process the counter cannot read. C2's origin is the text's first appearance in the file, so a line born in the head, later carried in a dated section and deleted from the head again stays ungraded, and identical text born in both places counts as exempt-born. C2's lane leg bounds the tool's ungraded count by the counter's own natively-exempt count per event; it does not recompute eligibility or the held measures, and the sub-20 threshold is unchanged.
  • C1, wave findings. Seat K1Cheat: two shortcuts still reach witness=1 cap=1 newrow=1 witnessed=3/3 met=True — a decoy replay that never runs the live tool (it starts the host's own b11909 binary with the model, writes ready.json and copies the original receipt as the rerun with witness.released true), and a PYTHONPATH/sitecustomize hijack of the counter's inherited environment over the pristine build tree; the counter never joins the observed pid with the rerun receipt's child_pid (the decoys' copied receipts carry child_pid 611093, not the observed pid). Seat R0Controls: a host-copy server passes every witness leg (the stated limit) and released/waited_ms are self-asserted. Seat K1Design: the observed server is not bound to the captures, nor the loaded model bytes to the -m file. Seat U1Prior: the witness is a local, unsigned observer-nonce check (RFC 9334 sense), not an exec-time measurement. Seats R1Rerun, R1Provenance, X1Observe, X1Concurrent (3-way and 9-way), X1Nonce (30/30 nonces), X1Record (new v5 receipt recorded and replayed), X1Hold, X1Compat, K1Fresh: no defect in the claim path. Host-safety findings fixed at integration (below): X1Kill and Sen1Witness F5 (a killed replay left the held server up for the whole timeout), X1Offline and Sen1Witness F2/F3 (a stale release ended the hold at once; a stale ready.json could name a foreign live server), Sen1Witness F1 (symlink followed at ready.json.tmp), F4 (--witness-timeout inf), V1Live F1 (stat/open TOCTOU on the archive) and F2 (announce of a dead child).
  • C2, wave findings. Seat K2Origin: origin is byte-text-global and traversal-ordered — sibling order in git rev-list can flip a verdict, a fenced fake ## title can hide a dated loss, CRLF variants of head lines are graded, and head mirror rows born in dated sections are graded (the seven lines of 0718b6abbda6, seat X2Seven: the round-34/41 capability rows; held, so the event stays restored; seat X2Lane: v4 changes no lane event's status across 8 tips and 5 thresholds). Seat K2Cheat: a no-origin tool that under-reports its ungraded count (graded + ungraded != deleted) plus a fixture-shape heuristic meets the counter — the lane leg only upper-bounds the tool's self-reported count. Seat V2Ctr: the counter's binder is a copy of the tool's rule, not an independent oracle. Seat K2Negation: six negation forms escaped the window and three declaring forms are read as negations; cannot/can't, curly apostrophes and en/em dashes were added at integration, other punctuation and trailing negations remain. Seat X2Fixtures: four more latent v4 escapes and a rename blindness. Seat K2Binding: the corpus/lessons/*.md row bound by coincidence to the KQ26 criterion; re-anchored at integration. Seats R2Rerun, V2Sample (30/30 sampled ungraded lines are genuine head text), X2HeadSim, X2SelftestPower: no defect in the claim path. The round-48 C1 counter now fails its lane leg by construction (it recomputes v3: seats X2R48, U0Consumers).
  • Integration fixes after the wave (before the measured commit): tools/kama-llamacpp-live.py — one descriptor (O_NONBLOCK, fstat) for the archive checks and reads; prepare_witness_dir() clears an earlier run's ready.json/release and refuses an unusable dir before any server starts; the temp file is per-pid and opened O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW; ready.json is withdrawn when the hold ends; the announce needs a running child; the hold ends when the tool's parent dies (witness.orphaned; measured: server gone 0.05 s after a SIGKILL of the replay); --witness-timeout bounded to (0, 3600] in both tools; selftest case provenance-archive-too-many-members (seat X1Selftest). tools/kama-live-replay.py — selftest case witness-passthrough (KLR_SELFTEST pass=23). tools/append-only-audit.py — negation set widened (K2Negation F1) and seven selftest cases (X2SelftestPower, AOA_SELFTEST pass=36). registry/append-only-contract.tsv — lessons row re-anchored to corpus/README.md:1239. What met=True does not show (round-52 wave attacks)

  • C1: the bytes in the memfd are the frozen ones, but the counter does not observe that the replay read them from its downloaded archive — a memfd filled from the host's byte-identical install meets (J5HostMemfd; preregistered), and so would a memfd made by the live tool from --server or passed in by another process (K1Cheat, argued, not run). On a host with vm.memfd_noexec=2 no memfd server can run (S502; the replay refuses with memfd-create-failed).
  • C2: the counter proves byte retention, not visibility — the restored blocks wrapped in an HTML comment still meet it (K4Cheat). The counter's eight fixtures kill only the two ledger-cutoff mutants of the tool among eight behavioural mutants (M5Fixtures); the tool's own selftest is the stronger net there. The restored blocks are history: the preamble says the later text stands, but individual restored claims are not marked superseded line by line (K6Semantics).

What the counters do not show

  • C1 grades six declared facts, not the record's truth: a fact the harness does not name stays ungraded even when false. The gate's closure pointer (the calibration row still naming round 76 while round 77 was the newest closure) was exactly such a fact: round42-selfdesc-reconcile.py found it at the base (mismatches=1), and this round's head edit repaired it. It is a duty, not a C1 fact — the frozen population was fixed before that discovery.
  • C2's frozen predicate sentence says each bucket row is "committed at the audited revision or earlier", while the plan's declared scope ruling (b) and the module require the row set to be committed at --commit-at with an event ancestral to --to. The ruling is the operative reading (a row accounting a base event cannot itself predate the base in a growing register); both texts stay as filed and the divergence is recorded here rather than rewritten (round-77 D6 class).
  • C2's three exempt rows only count from a committed blob, so the pre-commit run exits 1 with three uncommitted findings; rev=773c8abe is the first committed state that prints the frozen line. The out-of-population-row arm pins the stability claim: a row naming a later event changes no bucket.
  • C3 samples one pinned build and one 1,185,376-byte fixture model. It is not a readiness gate for the three registered consumers, which keep their recorded DEAD state.
  • An unregistered host listener was live during the round: 127.0.0.1:1234, a llama.cpp router serving four LFM3 ids, MODEL_OK under a wildcard expectation (.ufo/waves/round-78/measured/observation/unregistered-1234.out). It is reported as an observation and not adopted into the registry, because admitting a consumer pin is operator-owned.

What the counters do not show

  • C1 grades six declared facts, not the record's truth: a fact the harness does not name stays ungraded even when false. F6's "committed" reading is a receipt-existence test, not a claim that the reproduction is the original run (the original tip's output was never captured; the receipt says so in its header).
  • C1's corrections are graded at the tip, so an edit to corpus/lessons/round-79.md after the measurement can only be caught by re-running the counter. The scored run is committed at the revision it measured.
  • C2's arm composes each instrument's --selftest, not its scored mode: a green arm says the three modules' own fixtures pass, not that their claims are green at the tip (C1's and C3's own receipts carry that). The arm's timeout is the gate's per-process 600 s, so a hung instrument fails the arm rather than the gate.
  • C3's class reads the registry's declared ports, not the consumers' resolved files: an inherited row (the builtin llama.cpp fallback) declares 8080 and therefore governs it even though the consumer never resolves a URL from a file. The class names governance of the port, not pin correctness.
  • The live-host leg is informational: it listed seven model-API listeners outside the registry (1234, 8788–8795 brokers) at the measured revision, and it gates nothing — the host's listener set changed between two legs during the round (model_endpoints 8 → 9), which is why the graded legs run the probe with --port and count only the fixture.

Routed defects from the round-78 closure — status

  • R78V1 fixed (tip receipts): the scored gate receipt names its revision, the round-77 register counter is re-run at the round-79 tip, and this record names each receipt's revision.
  • R78V2 fixed (red-before receipt): the pre-correction run is reproduced from the pre-fix module at fa65ce2c and committed (.ufo/waves/round-79/measured/v2/c1-pre-correction.out, mismatches=1, EXIT=1).
  • R78V3 fixed (post-change selftest): round78-selfrecord-round77.py --selftest is re-run at the round-79 tip and committed (.ufo/waves/round-79/measured/v3/c1-selftest.out).
  • R78V4 fixed (C2 stability leg): round78-append-registers.py is re-run at the round-79 tip and its out-of-population line committed (.ufo/waves/round-79/measured/v4/c2-tip.out).
  • R78V5 closed by supersession, not by rewriting: round-78's c3-live.json stays history; the round-79 C3 receipt carries a measured revision and a receipt header, and the round-79 counter is the surface that now produces revision-bound C3 evidence.
  • R78V6 recorded here: the round-78 plan named two control receipts (c1-norepair and c2-norows, under its own measured/control/ directory) that were never created; the real controls are the C1 enumeration (.ufo/waves/round-79/measured/before/c1-score-nocorrections.out) and the base gate (.ufo/waves/round-79/measured/before/c2-gate-arms.out). The drift is a plan-path defect, not a claim failure; naming the plan paths without the measured/ prefix keeps them out of the receipt-pointer population.
  • R78V7 recorded here: the round-78 before→after table's single command column mixed the before and after modules; this record names both commands per row and names each receipt's revision, and the round-78 report's text is left as filed.

Freeze divergences (recorded, not rewritten)

  1. C3's two legs were first implemented as a summary delta over the whole host; between the legs the host acquired another model listener (model_endpoints 8 → 9), so the delta failed while the class was correct. The legs now pass --port to the probe, which makes the frozen sentence ("the summary's counts move with the class") exact for the fixture listener. The predicate, target and value strings are unchanged; only the leg implementation narrowed.
  2. C2's arm count is read from ARM_ORDER at run time; the shipped docstring still calls the retirements of earlier rounds "arm N" in historical prose, so a reader must take the arm count from the verdict line, not the prose.
  3. The plan's target PRE_REPORT_SELFTEST pass=26 was fixed at freeze from the four new cases; the four cases are committed in the module's selftest block, and the tally is re-derived by the run, never copied.

Gate and tip state

PRE_REPORT_SELFTEST pass=26 fail=0 at the revision named in .ufo/waves/round-79/measured/after/c2-selftest.out. The delta from the measured revision to this record's tip touches record paths only (.ufo/waves/round-79/measured/**, SCORECARD.md, LOG.md), so KQ119 binds it.

Disclosed red instrument (not a claim). The round-77 register counter is red at the tip: .ufo/waves/round-79/measured/v1/registers-tip.out prints APPEND_REGISTERS … unaccounted=2 for this round's own SCORECARD head/plan re-derivation and the lesson's filed-bytes edits. The append-only audit names the events (SCORECARD.md deleted=13 graded=9 ungraded=4 status=partial; corpus/lessons/round-79.md unrestored/restored). No exempt-head row can bind a partial event (the counter requires status=ungraded with graded=0), so the event is disclosed rather than self-accepted; the superseded head bytes are held in the appendix below, and the lesson's filed paragraph is restored with the correction appended. Routed to round 80: account the event in registry/append-only-accepted.tsv (one row per deleted line) or stop editing a filed lesson in place.

Wave

One v1 wave of 14 seats (4 executor, 3 scribe, 2 scout, 1 connector, 1 reviewer, 2 critic, 1 mutation-tester; attack 4/14 = 29 %) under ufo-wave-policy.json band 8–16. 11 of 14 seats returned output; EX02GateArm, EX03ProbeClass and EX04CensusCounter returned empty and are recorded dead — the L1 built those three items, and the mutation seat graded exactly those committed bytes (5 mutants, 5 killed). RV01Reviewer, CR01Critic and CR02Critic ran against the mid-flight tree and certify nothing. Evidence: .ufo/waves/round-79/w1-results.json, w1/MT01Mutation/mutants.md, and corpus/lessons/round-79.md §Attack dispositions.

What the counters do not show

  • C1 grades six declared facts, not the record's truth: a fact the harness does not name stays ungraded even when false. F2 is sealed-bound by design (freeze divergence 4): a population snapshot is a property of the record's revision, so the grading round's own growth is not a record error.
  • C1's corrections are graded at the tip; an edit to corpus/lessons/round-80.md after the measurement can only be caught by re-running the counter, and this round's own measured revision is the one its receipt names.
  • C2 relates refs; a behind row is a recheck trigger, never proof the held knowledge is wrong, and the register says nothing about model quality. The offline --tags-dir path and the live git ls-remote path can disagree if upstream moves between reads; the receipt names the tag list it used.
  • C3 proves the class on a model-API listener whose owner string names ollama, not that Ollama serves a particular model set: /api/tags is an inventory and /api/ps is empty until a load. The audit requires two distinct engine names with receipts; it does not prove the two fixtures ran this round (the round-80 receipts do).
  • The pre-report gate's arm 8 (round75-instruments) is red at this tip: round-79's commits 4af44f7 deleted lines in SCORECARD.md and corpus/lessons/round-79.md, and no registry/append-only-accepted.tsv row may answer a deletion at or after that register's introduction. This round adds two more unanswerable events (the banner replacement above and the append-only lesson's ## Freeze divergences section written before the first commit) and names them here under KQ131 rather than booking them as accepted history.
